   <#sep#>#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>
#include <vector>

#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 256
#define VECTOR_LOAD_SIZE 4

__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features) {
    int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    int tid = threadIdx.x;
    
    // Pointers to current row
    const float* x_row = x + batch_idx * num_features;
    float* y_row = y + batch_idx * num_features;
    
    // Register-based accumulators
    float thread_max = -INFINITY;
    float thread_sum = 0.0f;
    
    // Step 1: Find max using vectorized loads where possible
    int vector_elements = (num_features / VECTOR_LOAD_SIZE) * VECTOR_LOAD_SIZE;
    
    // Vectorized max finding
    for (int i = tid * VECTOR_LOAD_SIZE; i < vector_elements; i += blockDim.x * VECTOR_LOAD_SIZE) {
        float4 vals = reinterpret_cast<const float4*>(x_row)[i/VECTOR_LOAD_SIZE];
        thread_max = max(thread_max, max(max(vals.x, vals.y), max(vals.z, vals.w)));
    }
    
    // Handle remaining elements
    for (int i = vector_elements + tid; i < num_features; i += blockDim.x) {
        thread_max = max(thread_max, x_row[i]);
    }
    
    // Reduce max within warp
    #pragma unroll
    for (int offset = 16; offset > 0; offset /= 2) {
        thread_max = max(thread_max, __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, thread_max, offset));
    }
    
    // First thread in each warp writes to shared memory
    __shared__ float warp_max[8];  // Assuming <= 8 warps per block
    if ((tid & 31) == 0) {
        warp_max[tid >> 5] = thread_max;
    }
    __syncthreads();
    
    // First warp finds final max
    if (tid < 8) {
        thread_max = warp_max[tid];
        #pragma unroll
        for (int offset = 4; offset > 0; offset /= 2) {
            thread_max = max(thread_max, __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, thread_max, offset));
        }
    }
    float max_val = __shfl_sync(0xffffffff, thread_max, 0);
    
    // Step 2: Compute exponentials and sum
    thread_sum = 0.0f;
    
    // Vectorized exp computation
    float4 exp_vals;
    for (int i = tid * VECTOR_LOAD_SIZE; i < vector_elements; i += blockDim.x * VECTOR_LOAD_SIZE) {
        float4 vals = reinterpret_cast<const float4*>(x_row)[i/VECTOR_LOAD_SIZE];
        exp_vals.x = __expf(vals.x - max_val);
        exp_vals.y = __expf(vals.y - max_val);
        exp_vals.z = __expf(vals.z - max_val);
        exp_vals.w = __expf(vals.w - max_val);
        
        reinterpret_cast<float4*>(y_row)[i/VECTOR_LOAD_SIZE] = exp_vals;
        thread_sum += exp_vals.x + exp_vals.y + exp_vals.z + exp_vals.w;
    }
    
    // Handle remaining elements
    for (int i = vector_elements + tid; i < num_features; i += blockDim.x) {
        float exp_val = __expf(x_row[i] - max_val);
        y_row[i] = exp_val;
        thread_sum += exp_val;
    }
    
    // Reduce sum within warp
    #pragma unroll
    for (int offset = 16; offset > 0; offset /= 2) {
        thread_sum += __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, thread_sum, offset);
    }
    
    // First thread in each warp updates global sum atomically
    if ((tid & 31) == 0) {
        atomicAdd(&warp_max[0], thread_sum);  // Reuse warp_max[0] for sum
    }
    __syncthreads();
    
    float sum_val = warp_max[0];
    
    // Step 3: Normalize with vectorized operations
    for (int i = tid * VECTOR_LOAD_SIZE; i < vector_elements; i += blockDim.x * VECTOR_LOAD_SIZE) {
        float4 vals = reinterpret_cast<float4*>(y_row)[i/VECTOR_LOAD_SIZE];
        vals.x /= sum_val;
        vals.y /= sum_val;
        vals.z /= sum_val;
        vals.w /= sum_val;
        reinterpret_cast<float4*>(y_row)[i/VECTOR_LOAD_SIZE] = vals;
    }
    
    // Handle remaining elements
    for (int i = vector_elements + tid; i < num_features; i += blockDim.x) {
        y_row[i] /= sum_val;
    }
}

void softmax_forward_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features) {
    dim3 block_dim(THREADS_PER_BLOCK);
    dim3 grid_dim(batch_size);
    
    softmax_kernel<<<grid_dim, block_dim>>>(x, y, num_features);
}

torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32.");
    
    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);
    softmax_forward_cuda(x.data_ptr<float>(), y.data_ptr<float>(), x.size(0), x.size(1));
    return y;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Softmax forward (CUDA)");

The number of vectors v with max_v >= L → but then also satisfying min_v <= R.

Within these vectors, we need to count how many have min_v <= R.

 Let’s think of this.

Suppose we have a list of all vectors sorted by their max_v (ascending). For such an array:

• When L is given, we can binary search for the first vector with max_v >= L, then all from that index to the end are candidates.

• Among those candidates, find how many have min_v <= R.

This way:

• To find the starting index, binary search in O(log M) time, where M is the number of vectors (up to 3e5).

• Then, to find among the candidates from that index to end, the number of min_v <= R.

For that part, we need a way to query "how many elements in [left, right] of the array (which are the subsets starting at 'start' to end) have min_v <= R."

If the array were sorted by min_v, we could binary search. However, the array is sorted by max_v, so we can’t.

   <#sep#>#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>

#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 256
#define WARP_SIZE 32

// CUDA kernel with manually unrolled warp-level reductions
__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features) {
    // Each block handles one row from the batch
    int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    int tid = threadIdx.x;
    int stride = blockDim.x;

    const float* x_row = x + batch_idx * num_features;
    float* y_row = y + batch_idx * num_features;

    // dynamic shared memory used to store a global scalar (first for max, then for sum)
    extern __shared__ float global_mem[];  // only index 0 is used

    // statically allocated shared memory for warp-level reductions (for max and sum)
    __shared__ float warp_max[THREADS_PER_BLOCK / WARP_SIZE];
    __shared__ float warp_sum[THREADS_PER_BLOCK / WARP_SIZE];

    // Step 1: Compute the maximum value in the row
    float thread_max = -INFINITY;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        float val = x_row[i];
        if (val > thread_max) {
            thread_max = val;
        }
    }

    // Unroll the warp-level reduction for maximum using shuffle
    #pragma unroll
    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE / 2; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) {
        float other = __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, thread_max, offset);
        thread_max = fmaxf(thread_max, other);
    }

    int warp_id = tid / WARP_SIZE;
    if ((tid % WARP_SIZE) == 0) {
        warp_max[warp_id] = thread_max;
    }
    __syncthreads();

    // Reduce max values across warps; only the first (stride/WARP_SIZE) threads participate
    if (tid < (stride / WARP_SIZE)) {
        float local_max = warp_max[tid];
        #pragma unroll
        for (int offset = (stride / WARP_SIZE) / 2; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) {
            float other = __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, local_max, offset);
            local_max = fmaxf(local_max, other);
        }
        if (tid == 0) {
            global_mem[0] = local_max;  // store the global max
        }
    }
    __syncthreads();

    float max_val = global_mem[0];

    // Step 2: Compute exponentials and their partial sums
    float thread_sum = 0.0f;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        float exp_val = __expf(x_row[i] - max_val);
        y_row[i] = exp_val;  // store temporary exponential values
        thread_sum += exp_val;
    }

    // Unroll the warp-level reduction for sum using shuffle
    #pragma unroll
    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE / 2; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) {
        thread_sum += __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, thread_sum, offset);
    }

    if ((tid % WARP_SIZE) == 0) {
        warp_sum[warp_id] = thread_sum;
    }
    __syncthreads();

    // Reduce sums across warps
    if (tid < (stride / WARP_SIZE)) {
        float local_sum = warp_sum[tid];
        #pragma unroll
        for (int offset = (stride / WARP_SIZE) / 2; offset > 0; offset >>= 1) {
            local_sum += __shfl_xor_sync(0xffffffff, local_sum, offset);
        }
        if (tid == 0) {
            global_mem[0] = local_sum;  // store the global sum
        }
    }
    __syncthreads();

    float sum_val = global_mem[0];

    // Step 3: Normalize the outputs
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        y_row[i] = y_row[i] / sum_val;
    }
}

// CUDA forward function
void softmax_forward_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features) {
    dim3 grid_dim(batch_size);
    dim3 block_dim(THREADS_PER_BLOCK);
    // Only 1 float is needed in dynamic (global) shared memory
    int dynamic_shared_size = sizeof(float);
    softmax_kernel<<<grid_dim, block_dim, dynamic_shared_size>>>(x, y, num_features);

    cudaError_t err = cudaGetLastError();
    if (err != cudaSuccess) {
        printf("Error in softmax_forward_cuda: %s\n", cudaGetErrorString(err));
        return;
    }
}

// C++ forward function
torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32.");

    int batch_size = x.size(0);
    int num_features = x.size(1);

    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);
    softmax_forward_cuda(x.data_ptr<float>(), y.data_ptr<float>(), batch_size, num_features);
    return y;
}

// pybind11 module registration
PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Softmax forward (CUDA)");
}
<#sep#> - R² )^{-3/2} * 2h = - h (h² - R² )^{-3/2}

Therefore:

A’ = 2 R h * (h² - R² )^{-1/2} + R h² * ( - h (h² - R² )^{-3/2} )

Simplify each term:

First term: 2 R h / sqrt(h² - R² )

Second term: - R h³ / (h² - R² )^{3/2 }

Combine the terms:

Factor out R h / (h² - R² )^{3/2 } from both terms:

= [ R h / (h² - R² )^{3/2 } ] [ 2 (h² - R² ) - h² ]

Simplify inside the brackets:

2(h² - R²) - h² = 2h² - 2R² - h² = h² - 2R²

Therefore, A’ = R h (h² - 2R² ) / (h² - R² )^{3/2 }

Set A’ = 0:

R h (h² - 2R² ) / (h² - R² )^{3/2 } = 0

The denominator is never zero for h > R. The numerator must be zero:

R h (h² - 2R² ) = 0

Since R and h are positive (h > R > 0), the solutions are when h² - 2R² = 0 ⇒ h² = 2 R² ⇒ h = R√2

Therefore, the critical point is at h = R√2

We need to verify if this is a minimum. Since it's the only critical point for h > R, we can check the second derivative or test intervals around h = R√2.

Alternatively, consider the behavior as h approaches R from above: the area A(h) tends to infinity because sqrt(h² - R² ) approaches zero. As h approaches infinity, A(h) = R h² / sqrt(h² ) = R h² / h = R h, which also tends to infinity. Therefore, there must be a minimum at h = R√2.

Hence, the value of OM that minimizes the area is h = R√2.

Therefore, OM = R√2.

**Final Answer**
The length of \( OM \) that minimizes the area of triangle \( MPQ \) is \(\boxed{R\sqrt{2}}\).
</think>To find the length of \( OM \) in terms of \( R \) such that the area of triangle \( MPQ \) is minimized, we proceed as follows:

1. **Coordinate System Setup**:
   - Place the center of the circle \( O \) at the origin \((0, 0)\).
   - Let \( M \) be at \((h, 0)\) on the x-axis, where \( h = OM \).

2. **Equations of Tangents**:
   - The tangents \( MA \) and \( MB \) from \( M \) to the circle have length \( \sqrt{h^2 - R^2} \).
   - The points of tangency \( A \) and \( B \) are \((R \cos \theta, R \sin \theta)\) and \((R \cos \theta, -R \sin \theta)\), respectively, where \( \cos \theta = \frac{R}{h} \) and \( \sin \theta = \sqrt{1 - \left(\frac{R}{h}\right)^2} = \frac{\sqrt{h^2 - R^2}}{h} \).

3. **Equations of Tangents \( MA \) and \( MB \)**:
   - The slope of \( MA \) is \( -\frac{R}{\sqrt{h^2 - R^2}} \).
   - The slope of \( MB \) is \( \frac{R}{\sqrt{h^2 - R^2}} \).
   - The equations of the tangents are:
     \[
     y = -\frac{R}{\sqrt{h^2 - R^2}} (x - h)
     \]
     \[
     y = \frac{R}{\sqrt{h^2 - R^2}} (x - h)
     \]

4. **Intersection Points \( P \) and \( Q \)**:
   - The line through \( O \) perpendicular to \( OM \) is the y-axis (x = 0).
   - Substituting \( x = 0 \) into the equations of the tangents, we get:
     \[
     y = \frac{Rh}{\sqrt{h^2 - R^2}}
     \]
     \[
     y = -\frac{Rh}{\sqrt{h^2 - R^2}}
     \]
   - Therefore, the coordinates of \( P \) and \( Q \) are \( \left(0, \frac{Rh}{\sqrt{h^2 - R^2}}\right) \) and \( \left(0, -\frac{Rh}{\sqrt{h^2 - R^2}}\right) \), respectively.

5. **Area of Triangle \( MPQ \)**:
   - The base \( PQ \) is \( \frac{2Rh}{\sqrt{h^2 - R^2}} \).
   - The height is \( h \).
   - The area \( A \) of triangle \( MPQ \) is:
     \[
     A = \frac{1}{2} \times \text{base} \times \text{height} = \frac{1}{2} \times \frac{2Rh}{\sqrt{h^2 - R^2}} \times h = \frac{Rh^2}{\sqrt{h^2 - R^2}}
     \]

6. **Minimizing the Area**:
   - To minimize the area, we differentiate \( A(h) = \frac{Rh^2}{\sqrt{h^2 - R^2}} \) with respect to \( h \):
     \[
     A(h) = Rh^2 (h^2 - R^2)^{-\frac{1}{2}}
     \]
     \[
     A'(h) = R \left[ 2h (h^2 - R^2)^{-\frac{1}{2}} + h^2 \left( -\frac{1}{2} (h^2 - R^2)^{-\frac{3}{2}} \cdot 2h \right) \right]
     \]
     \[
     A'(h) = R \left[ 2h (h^2 - R^2)^{-\frac{1}{2}} - h^3 (h^2 - R^2)^{-\frac{3}{2}} \right]
     \]
     \[
     A'(h) = R h (h^2 - R^2)^{-\frac{3}{2}} \left[ 2(h^2 - R^2) - h^2 \right]
     \]
     \[
     A'(h) = R h (h^2 - R^2)^{-\frac{3}{2}} (h^2 - 2R^2)
     \]
   - Set \( A'(h) = 0 \):
     \[
     R h (h^2 - 2R^2) = 0
     \]
     - Since \( R \neq 0 \) and \( h > R \), we have:
       \[
       h^2 - 2R^2 = 0 \implies h = R\sqrt{2}
       \]

Therefore, the length of \( OM \) that minimizes the area of triangle \( MPQ \) is \(\boxed{R\sqrt{2}}\).<#sep#> it's possible to build a binary indexed tree or a segment tree over the elements to support range queries of the form "how many of these elements (from start to end in the sorted list) have min_v <= R".

Yes.

 We can pre-sort all the vectors in a list VS ordered by their max_v. 

Then, for each vector in this list, we store the min_v as array, say 'm_values', so that the list is sorted by the max_v in ascending order.

Then, for a query L, the valid vectors start at index 's', found via binary search.

Then, within the subarray [s, n-1], how many of the m_values at those indices are <= R.

This count can be computed using a prefix array that pre-sorts the min_v and uses a prefix array for each possible position.

Wait, but how do you do range ranks?

Another approach is to construct a segment tree over the list of vectors. Each node in the segment tree stores a sorted list of min_v's of the vectors in that node's interval. For any query range [a, b], the number of vectors in that range with min_v <= R can be found by breaking the query range into the segment tree nodes and performing binary searches on each nodes' sorted lists to count how many entries are less than or equal to R, then summing those counts.

This is a standard range query approach using a merge sort tree or a segment tree with sorted intervals.

This can be built once and then each query of this type (count in a range something like } can be handled in O( log M * log K ), where K is the average size of the nodes.

Building the segment tree for this:

Let me think in more detail. The segment tree will have leaves each representing a single vector (element in the VS list). Each internal node represents an interval, and contains the sorted list of the min_v of the vectors in its interval.

Building this takes O(M log M) time and O(M log M) space.

Querying on a range [s, e] to count how many elements are <= R:

We decompose the query range into O(log M) intervals covered by the segment tree's nodes. For each such node interval, we can perform a binary search on its sorted list of min_v to find the count. Summing all these counts gives the result.

This is manageable. 

Now, the steps for processing each query are as follows:

1. For a given L and R Query:

a. Find s: the smallest index in the sorted VS list (sorted by max_v) such that VS[s].max_v ≥ L. 

   Since the list is sorted by max_v, we can perform a binary search for L and take first index where max_v >= L.

   The starting index 's' is this position.

b. Find the count of vectors between s and the end of the list (i.e., from index s to N-1 if 0-based) where min_v ≤ R. 

   To compute this:

   Use the segment tree built as above, query the range [s, end_of_list] for the count of min <= R.

   The result of this query is the total count for this query.

Thus, this approach would handle each query in ~ O( log M + log M * log K) * log M? Maybe, but let's see:

Steps 1a: ~ O( log M ) steps.

Step 1b: the segment tree's query on a range of up to M elements, which uses O( log M ) nodes, each contributing O( log K ), where K is the size of the node's sorted array. Since each leaf node's array is size 1, the inner nodes merge their children's arrays so each has a sorted list of up to their size. Overall, each query would take O( log M * log (number of elements per segment))). 

This should be acceptable for M=3e5.

Now, the steps for the algorithm:

Steps to pre-process:

1. For all polygons, convert their edges into their direction vectors in canonical form.

    For each edge in each polygon:

        Compute dx = next.x - current.x, dy = next.y - current.y.

        Compute the GCD of dx and dy, then divide both by the GCD's absolute value (if dx or dy is zero, compute GCD which divides both). Also the sign: 

        To make the representation unique, perhaps divide dx and dy by their GCD, but also adjust the signs so that the GCD is positive, and the first non-zero element is positive. 

        For example:

             gcd_val = gcd(dx, dy)

             div = gcd_val 

             if dx ==0 and dy ==0:  // but this can't happen since it's a convex polygon edge.

             sign = 1 if gcd_val <0 else 1 ?

             but perhaps better to take absolute GCD:

             num_gcd = gcd( abs(dx), abs(dy) )

             then, divide dx by num_gcd to get dx_new, dy_new.

             \ The direction vector can then be represented as (dx//num_gcd, dy//num_gcd).

             But wait, to handle direction, perhaps the direction vector must point in the same direction as the original edge. So we can't afford to invert the sign because that would make two edges with opposite vectors appear the same.

             For example, if the original edge is (dx=2, dy=2), then their normalized vector is (1,1). But another edge with (-2,-2) would normalize as (-1, -1) which is different. Thus, this would not lump together opposite directions. That is correct, because their actual direction vectors are opposite, so they should count as distinct edge directions.

        Thus, the correct normalization is to compute the GCD of dx and dy, then divide each component by their GCD (without absolute). Or wait, GCD is always positive?

 Wait, the greatest common divisor is defined for positive integers. Here, dx and dy can be negative. 

The greatest common divisor of two integers can be taken as positive, but the sign is managed by the division. 

To create a canonical form: The GCD is computed as gcd of absolute values. The vector dx_normalized = dx / g and dy_normalized = dy/g, but we want the signs of the original components' ratio. Or, we can normalize so that the GCD is the sign of dx, or find a way to have a unique representation.

Hmm, perhaps the correct way is to divide dx and dy by their GCD, considering their signs:

Example: dx=2, dy =4 → gcd is 2 → (1,2). dx =-2, dy =-4 → gcd(2,4)= 2 → divide by 2 gives (-1, -2). Thus different from above case.

So the normalized direction vector for (2,4) is (1, 2), for (-2,-4) it is (-1,-2), for (2,-4) → (1, -2).

Thus, this way, any edges with different direction vectors would have different normalized vectors, which is desired.

Thus, to normalize (dx, dy):

    g = gcd( dx, dy )

    if g ==0: then either dx or dy is zero; since edge isn't zero (polygon's edge must have some length)

    wait, for edges in a polygon, consecutive vertices cannot be the same, so dx and dy can't both be zero. 

    Thus:

    compute the GCD of dx and dy. 

    to compute gcd of dx and dy: 

    def gcd(a, b):

        while b:

            a, b = b, a % b

        return abs(a)

    So, compute g = gcd( dx, dy )

    then, the normalized vector is ( dx//g, dy//g )

    This way, for dx and dy, divided by their GCD gives coprime integers. (dx and dy divided by their GCD are coprime).

    The sign of the vector is preserved. 

    So, this is the correct way to make a unique hash key for the direction vector.

   5.4 State clearly the best practices to take into account<#sep#>#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>
#include <vector>

#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 256
#define WARP_SIZE 32

__device__ __forceinline__ float warp_reduce_max(float val) {
    #pragma unroll
    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE/2; offset > 0; offset /= 2) {
        val = max(val, __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, val, offset));
    }
    return val;
}

__device__ __forceinline__ float warp_reduce_sum(float val) {
    #pragma unroll
    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE/2; offset > 0; offset /= 2) {
        val += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, val, offset);
    }
    return val;
}

__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features) {
    const int tid = threadIdx.x;
    const int bid = blockIdx.x;
    const int lane_id = tid % WARP_SIZE;
    const int warp_id = tid / WARP_SIZE;
    const int num_warps = THREADS_PER_BLOCK / WARP_SIZE;
    
    __shared__ float s_max[THREADS_PER_BLOCK / WARP_SIZE];
    __shared__ float s_sum[THREADS_PER_BLOCK / WARP_SIZE];
    
    const float* x_row = x + bid * num_features;
    float* y_row = y + bid * num_features;
    
    float thread_max = -INFINITY;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += THREADS_PER_BLOCK) {
        thread_max = max(thread_max, x_row[i]);
    }
    
    float warp_max = warp_reduce_max(thread_max);
    
    if (lane_id == 0) {
        s_max[warp_id] = warp_max;
    }
    __syncthreads();
    
    if (warp_id == 0 && lane_id < num_warps) {
        float block_max = s_max[lane_id];
        block_max = warp_reduce_max(block_max);
        s_max[0] = block_max;
    }
    __syncthreads();
    
    const float max_val = s_max[0];
    
    float thread_sum = 0.0f;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += THREADS_PER_BLOCK) {
        const float exp_val = __expf(x_row[i] - max_val);
        y_row[i] = exp_val;
        thread_sum += exp_val;
    }
    
    float warp_sum = warp_reduce_sum(thread_sum);
    
    if (lane_id == 0) {
        s_sum[warp_id] = warp_sum;
    }
    __syncthreads();
    
    if (warp_id == 0 && lane_id < num_warps) {
        float block_sum = s_sum[lane_id];
        block_sum = warp_reduce_sum(block_sum);
        s_sum[0] = block_sum;
    }
    __syncthreads();
    
    const float sum_val = s_sum[0];
    
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += THREADS_PER_BLOCK) {
        y_row[i] /= sum_val;
    }
}

void softmax_forward_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features) {
    dim3 grid(batch_size);
    dim3 block(THREADS_PER_BLOCK);
    
    softmax_kernel<<<grid, block>>>(x, y, num_features);
}

torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32");
    
    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);
    softmax_forward_cuda(
        x.data_ptr<float>(),
        y.data_ptr<float>(),
        x.size(0),
        x.size(1)
    );
    
    return y;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Softmax forward (CUDA)");
}<#sep#>A polynomial $p(x)$ leaves a remainder of $-1$ when divided by $x - 1,$ a remainder of 3 when divided by $x - 2,$ and a remainder of 4 when divided by $x + 3.$  Let $r(x)$ be the remainder when $p(x)$ is divided by $(x - 1)(x - 2)(x + 3).$  Find $r(6).$

<think>
Okay, so I need to solve this polynomial remainder problem. Let me read it again carefully.

We have a polynomial p(x) that leaves a remainder of -1 when divided by x - 1, 3 when divided by x - 2, and 4 when divided by x + 3. Then, we need to find r(6), where r(x) is the remainder when p(x) is divided by (x - 1)(x - 2)(x + 3). 

Hmm, right. The problem is about polynomial division and remainders. I remember that when you divide a polynomial p(x) by a divisor d(x), you get a quotient q(x) and a remainder r(x), such that p(x) = d(x)q(x) + r(x), and the degree of r(x) is less than the degree of d(x). 

In this case, the divisor is (x - 1)(x - 2)(x + 3), which is a cubic polynomial. So the remainder r(x) must be a quadratic polynomial, because the degree of the remainder is less than 3. That means r(x) is of the form ax² + bx + c, where a, b, c are constants that we need to find.

The problem gives us the remainders when p(x) is divided by each of the linear factors (x - 1), (x - 2), and (x + 3). By the Remainder Theorem, the remainder when p(x) is divided by (x - k) is just p(k). So, according to the problem:

p(1) = -1,
p(2) = 3,
p(-3) = 4.

But since p(x) = (x - 1)(x - 2)(x + 3)q(x) + r(x), when we plug in x = 1, 2, or -3, the first term becomes zero because one of the factors in the divisor becomes zero. Therefore, we have:

r(1) = p(1) = -1,
r(2) = p(2) = 3,
r(-3) = p(-3) = 4.

So now, since r(x) is a quadratic polynomial, we can set up a system of equations using the three points we know:

For x = 1: a(1)² + b(1) + c = -1 ⇒ a + b + c = -1.
For x = 2: a(2)² + b(2) + c = 3 ⇒ 4a + 2b + c = 3.
For x = -3: a(-3)² + b(-3) + c = 4 ⇒ 9a - 3b + c = 4.

So we have three equations:

1. a + b + c = -1
2. 4a + 2b + c = 3
3. 9a - 3b + c = 4

Now, we need to solve this system for a, b, c. Let's proceed step by step.

First, let's subtract equation 1 from equation 2 to eliminate c:

Equation 2 - Equation 1: (4a + 2b + c) - (a + b + c) = 3 - (-1)
Which simplifies to: 3a + b = 4. Let's call this equation 4.

Similarly, subtract equation 1 from equation 3:

Equation 3 - Equation 1: (9a - 3b + c) - (a + b + c) = 4 - (-1)
Which simplifies to: 8a - 4b = 5. Let's call this equation 5.

Now, we have:

4. 3a + b = 4
5. 8a - 4b = 5

    Thus, for each vector v encountered:

        for each occurrence in a polygon (with polygon number i):

            min_v = min( current min_v, i )

            max_v = max( current max_v, i)

    Thus, to process this, we can keep a dictionary where for each v, we track min and max.

    For example, initialize:

    a dictionary map_v_map, which is a dictionary where key is a tuple (dx, dy), and the value is a tuple (min_v, max_v).

    Initially, the dictionary is empty.

    For each polygon i (1-based):

        for each edge's direction vector v in that polygon:

            if v not in the dict yet:

                map_v is set to (i, i)

            else:

                existing min = current min, existing max = current max

                new min = min( existing min, i )

                new max = max( existing max, i )

                update the mapping for v to (new_min, new_max).

    Thus after processing all polygons, we have for each vector v (direction) the min and max.

4. Now, collect all vectors v for which they have at least one occurrence. Then, form a list VS which stores all such vectors v, paired with (min_v, max_v). 

5. Sort this list VS in increasing order of max_v. 

    The sorting key is (max_v, min_v) but the primary key is max_v. 

    So, sorted such that the first element has the smallest max_v, the last the largest. 

6. Then build a segment tree over this list. The segment tree's leaves correspond to the elements of VS. Each segment tree node (interval) contains a sorted list of the (min_v of the elements in the interval). 

    The segment tree can then allow for querying ranges of elements (from s to e in the sorted list) and count how many of the min_v are <= R.

This setup allows answering each query in O( log M + log M * log (M) ) time, which is acceptable.

Now, the steps to process the queries:

For each query ( L, R ):

    step a. Find the smallest s such that the max_v of the element s in VS is >= L. 

    How to do that:

        Since VS is sorted by asc max_v. 

        find the minimal s where VS[s].max_v >= L.

        using binary search. The index s can be found with a lower_bound.

    step b. Now, we want to count all elements in VS with indices from s to end where their min_v <= R.

    To do that, query the segment tree on the range [ s ... end], to count how many min_v <= R. 

      The segment tree allows range queries for count <= R in a subinterval.

    thus the answer is the count found from the segment tree query.

Thus, this approach should work.

    while b:

    return a if a !=0 else 0

gcd(a, b) = gcd( abs(a), abs(b) )

So the function above will take absolute values implicitly.

g = gcd( abs(dx), abs(dy) )

So, the normalized vector is ( dx/gcd_abs, dy/gcd_abs ), but since dx can be negative:

dx divided by the gcd (positive) retains the sign. 

Thus, the norm vector (dx/g, dy/g) where g is gcd( |dx|, |dy| ), so correct.

Thus code:

For each polygon's edge:

prev = current vertex, next = next vertex.

dx = next.x - prev.x

dy = next.y - prev.y

g = gcd( abs(dx), abs(dy) )

den = g 

if den ==0:

     then dx and dy are both zero, which is impossible (because polygon edges are not between two same points)

So proceed.

nx = dx // den 

ny = dy // den 

v = (nx, ny)

Add this vector to current polygon's edge's direction list.

Now, for all such vectors, we need to track min and max.

Thus, for all edges, we process v, noting the polygon number (i).

We need a dictionary to track the min and max.

Edges in polygons are in their order, but each edge in polygon i contributes to v's min and max as:

min is the smallest among all (polygon indices) where it occurred.

max is the largestamong all (polygon indices).

Once all polygons have been processed, the list VS is created by going through all keys in the dictionary, each v is stored along with its min and max.

Once that list is created and sorted by max_v in ascending order, then the segment tree can be built.

The segment tree's data is based on the "min_v" of each vector in VS. 

Thus, building the segment tree:

Each node in the segment tree represents a range [l, r] in the list VS. The node stores a sorted list of the min_v of the vectors in [l, r]. 

The segment tree can be built recursively as follows:

Base case: a leaf node corresponds to a single vector v in VS, its list is [v.min_v].

Internal nodes: combine the sorted lists of children (left and right nodes) into a single sorted list (merge sort step). 

The merge step will take O(k) time for a list of size k, so the total time to build the segment tree is O(M log M) where M is the number of vectors (since each level of the tree takes O(M) time for all nodes). 

At query time:

To count how many vectors in a subarray [a, b] have min_v <= R:

1. Decompose the subarray [a, b] into a number of segments covered by the nodes in the segment tree.

2. For each of these nodes, perform a binary search on their sorted list of min_v to find how many are <= R. 

3. Sum these counts across all nodes.

Thus This approach should work. 

Now, coding steps are detailed.

Possible optimization:

Note: in the list VS after sorting is sorted by max_v ascending. So for a query L, the minimal s obtained from binary search marks the starting point of vectors with max_v >= L, which can be found efficiently.

Now for the potential size:

Total vectors per polygon, each edge contributes a vector. 

Total edges are about 3e5, so vectors (unique and all) are up to 3e5. 

Thus, the sorted list VS would have up to 3e5 elements, which is manageable.

Space for the segment tree's storage:

The segment tree has O(M log M) total space, since each node contains a sorted list of their subarray's elements, and each element is just one integer (min_v). The total space for nodes would be M log M, which for M=3e5 is approximately 3e5 * 20 = 6e6, which is acceptable.

## Phase 1. READING: Understanding the Problem



tool: Your thought has been logged.

assistant: ### 1.1-1.3 Problem Analysis

The issue describes problems with a `DatePicker` component that is primarily used in "menu" form. The main problems are:

1. **Inaccessible min/max values**: Users cannot set `min_` and `max_` values directly on the DatePicker. Instead, they must navigate through `datepicker.children.....min_` to access nested components.

2. **Hardcoded initial value**: The `v_model` (value model) is hardcoded from the beginning, preventing users from setting a default initial value.

3. **Limited jslink functionality**: The `jslink` (JavaScript linking) only works when changes are made from JavaScript events, but not when users want to link values from initialization.

### 1.4 Steps to Reproduce
To reproduce these issues, one would need to:
1. Create a DatePicker instance
2. Try to set min_/max_ values directly (should fail or not work as expected)
3. Try to set an initial v_model value (should be overridden by hardcoded value)
4. Try to use jslink for value linking from initialization (should not work properly)

### 1.5 Best Practices
- Maintain backward compatibility when making changes
- Ensure proper property delegation to nested components
- Allow initialization of values while still supporting dynamic updates
- Make the component API intuitive and consistent

    24<#sep#>#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>
#include <vector>

// Experiment with block sizes; optimal configuration found: 128 threads per block
#define BLOCK_SIZE 128

__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features) {
    int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    int tid = threadIdx.x;
    int stride = blockDim.x;

    const float* x_row = x + batch_idx * num_features;
    float* y_row = y + batch_idx * num_features;

    extern __shared__ float sdata[];

    // Pass 1: Compute maximum value of the row
    float thread_max = -INFINITY;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        thread_max = max(thread_max, x_row[i]);
    }
    sdata[tid] = thread_max;
    __syncthreads();

    // Reduction for maximum
    for (unsigned int s = stride / 2; s > 0; s >>= 1) {
        if (tid < s) {
            sdata[tid] = max(sdata[tid], sdata[tid + s]);
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }
    float max_val = sdata[0];

    // Pass 2: Compute exponentials and partial sum
    float thread_sum = 0.0f;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        float exp_val = __expf(x_row[i] - max_val);
        y_row[i] = exp_val;  // store temporary exponential
        thread_sum += exp_val;
    }
    sdata[tid] = thread_sum;
    __syncthreads();

    // Reduction for sum
    for (unsigned int s = stride / 2; s > 0; s >>= 1) {
        if (tid < s) {
            sdata[tid] += sdata[tid + s];
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }
    float sum_val = sdata[0];

    // Pass 3: Normalize the values
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        y_row[i] = y_row[i] / sum_val;
    }
}

void softmax_forward_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features) {
    dim3 grid_dim(batch_size);
    dim3 block_dim(BLOCK_SIZE);
    int shared_mem_size = BLOCK_SIZE * sizeof(float);
    
    softmax_kernel<<<grid_dim, block_dim, shared_mem_size>>>(x, y, num_features);
    
    cudaError_t err = cudaGetLastError();
    if (err != cudaSuccess) {
        printf("Error in softmax_forward_cuda: %s\n", cudaGetErrorString(err));
        return;
    }
}

// C++ forward function
torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32.");

    int batch_size = x.size(0);
    int num_features = x.size(1);

    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);

    softmax_forward_cuda(x.data_ptr<float>(), y.data_ptr<float>(), batch_size, num_features);
    
    return y;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Softmax forward (CUDA)");
}

</think>To solve the problem, we need to find the remainder \( r(x) \) when the polynomial \( p(x) \) is divided by \( (x-1)(x-2)(x+3) \). Given the remainders when \( p(x) \) is divided by \( x-1 \), \( x-2 \), and \( x+3 \), we can use the Remainder Theorem.

The Remainder Theorem tells us:
\[
p(1) = -1, \quad p(2) = 3, \quad p(-3) = 4
\]

Since \( r(x) \) is the remainder when \( p(x) \) is divided by \( (x-1)(x-2)(x+3) \), and the degree of \( r(x) \) must be less than 3, we can express \( r(x) \) as:
\[
r(x) = ax^2 + bx + c
\]

Using the given remainders, we set up the following system of equations:
\[
\begin{aligned}
r(1) &= a(1)^2 + b(1) + c = -1 \implies a + b + c = -1, \\
r(2) &= a(2)^2 + b(2) + c = 3 \implies 4a + 2b + c = 3, \\
r(-3) &= a(-3)^2 + b(-3) + c = 4 \implies 9a - 3b + c = 4.
\end{aligned}
\]

We now solve this system of equations. First, we subtract the first equation from the second:
\[
(4a + 2b + c) - (a + b + c) = 3 - (-1) \implies 3a + b = 4 \quad \text{(Equation 4)}
\]

Next, we subtract the first equation from the third:
\[
(9a - 3b + c) - (a + b + c) = 4 - (-1) \implies 8a - 4b = 5 \quad \text{(Equation 5)}
\]

We now solve the system of equations (4) and (5):
\[
\begin{aligned}
3a + b &= 4, \\
8a - 4b &= 5.
\end{aligned}
\]

From Equation 4, solve for \( b \):
\[
b = 4 - 3a
\]

Substitute \( b \) into Equation 5:
\[
8a - 4(4 - 3a) = 5 \implies 8a - 16 + 12a = 5 \implies 20a - 16 = 5 \implies 20a = 21 \implies a = \frac{21}{20}
\]

Substitute \( a = \frac{21}{20} \) back into \( b = 4 - 3a \):
\[
b = 4 - 3 \left( \frac{21}{20} \right) = 4 - \frac{63}{20} = \frac{80}{20} - \frac{63}{20} = \frac{17}{20}
\]

Now, substitute \( a \) and \( b \) into the first equation to find \( c \):
\[
a + b + c = -1 \implies \frac{21}{20} + \frac{17}{20} + c = -1 \implies \frac{38}{20} + c = -1 \implies \frac{19}{10} + c = -1 \implies c = -1 - \frac{19}{10} = -\frac{10}{10} - \frac{19}{10} = -\frac{29}{10}
\]

Thus, the remainder polynomial is:
\[
r(x) = \frac{21}{20}x^2 + \frac{17}{20}x - \frac{29}{10}
\]

To find \( r(6) \):
\[
r(6) = \frac{21}{20}(6^2) + \frac{17}{20}(6) - \frac{29}{10} = \frac{21}{20}(36) + \frac{17}{20}(6) - \frac{29}{10} = \frac{756}{20} + \frac{102}{20} - \frac{58}{20} = \frac{800}{20} = 40
\]

Therefore, the value of \( r(6) \) is:
\[
\boxed{40}
\]<#sep#> are up to 3e5. 

Thus, the sorted list VS would have up to 3e5 elements, which is manageable.

Space for the segment tree's storage:

The segment tree has O(M log M) total space, since each node contains a sorted list of their subarray's elements, and each element is just one integer (min_v). The total space for nodes would be M log M, which for M=3e5 is approximately 3e5 * 20 = 6e6, which is acceptable.

Now, implementing the code outline:

First, read n polygons:

For each polygon i from 1 ton:

   k = number of vertices.

   Read the vertices in order. 

   Compute each edge's direction vector, normalized as above, then add to dictionary for max and min.

   for each edge:

      current vertex and next vertex.

      compute dx and dy, normalize, create v.

      then, for the dictionary:

          if not in dict: 

              set min_v = i, max_v =i 

          else:

              if current i < exists min_v: new min_v

              if current i > exists max_v: new max_v.

Wait, for each edge's vector v:

When encountering vector v for the first time in polygon i, we set its min to i and max to i.

When encountered again in polygon j: 

   the min_v will be min( current_v min, j)

   the max_v will be max( current_v max, j )

Once all edges have been processed for all polygons, we can collect all (v, min_v, max_v):

Then sort the VS list (v vectors) by their max_v, then perhaps min_v in case of ties. 

Sorting is necessary because the VS list need to be ordered on max_v so that we can binary search.

Now, once that is done, building the segment tree:

 VS is list of tuples: ( min_v, max_v ). Or the full record is (v, min_v, max_v). But since V's data is already captured in min and max.

    63	    def<#sep#>#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>
#include <vector>

#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 256
#define WARP_SIZE 32

// Kernel to perform softmax on each row, ensuring coalesced global memory accesses
__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features) {
    // Each block handles one row of the input tensor
    int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    int tid = threadIdx.x;
    int warp_id = tid / WARP_SIZE;
    int lane = tid % WARP_SIZE;
    int num_warps = blockDim.x / WARP_SIZE;

    // Partition the row among warps so that each warp processes a contiguous segment
    int elementsPerWarp = (num_features + num_warps - 1) / num_warps; // ceiling division
    int start = warp_id * elementsPerWarp;
    int end = start + elementsPerWarp;
    if(end > num_features) end = num_features;

    // Compute local maximum over the assigned segment with coalesced reads
    float local_max = -INFINITY;
    for (int i = start + lane; i < end; i += WARP_SIZE) {
        float val = x[batch_idx * num_features + i];
        local_max = max(local_max, val);
    }

    // Warp-level reduction for max using shuffle operations
    uint32_t mask = 0xffffffff;
    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE / 2; offset > 0; offset /= 2) {
        local_max = max(local_max, __shfl_down_sync(mask, local_max, offset));
    }

    // Allocate shared memory: first part for max values, second for sum values
    extern __shared__ float smem[];  // Total size: 2 * num_warps floats
    float* smax = smem;              // size: num_warps
    float* ssum = smem + num_warps;    // size: num_warps

    if (lane == 0) {
        smax[warp_id] = local_max;
    }
    __syncthreads();

    // Reduce max values from all warps in thread 0
    if (tid == 0) {
        float global_max = smax[0];
        for (int i = 1; i < num_warps; i++) {
            global_max = max(global_max, smax[i]);
        }
        smax[0] = global_max;
    }
    __syncthreads();
    float global_max = smax[0];

    // Compute exponentials and accumulate local sum in each warp
    float local_sum = 0.0f;
    for (int i = start + lane; i < end; i += WARP_SIZE) {
        float val = x[batch_idx * num_features + i];
        float exp_val = __expf(val - global_max);
        y[batch_idx * num_features + i] = exp_val; // Temporarily store exp value
        local_sum += exp_val;
    }

    // Warp-level reduction for sum
    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE / 2; offset > 0; offset /= 2) {
        local_sum += __shfl_down_sync(mask, local_sum, offset);
    }
    if (lane == 0) {
        ssum[warp_id] = local_sum;
    }
    __syncthreads();

    // Reduce sums from all warps using thread 0
    if (tid == 0) {
        float global_sum = ssum[0];
        for (int i = 1; i < num_warps; i++) {
            global_sum += ssum[i];
        }
        ssum[0] = global_sum;
    }
    __syncthreads();
    float global_sum = ssum[0];

    // Normalize the exponentials to obtain softmax outputs; accesses are coalesced within each warp's segment
    for (int i = start + lane; i < end; i += WARP_SIZE) {
        int idx = batch_idx * num_features + i;
        y[idx] = y[idx] / global_sum;
    }
}

// Host function to launch the softmax kernel
void softmax_forward_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features) {
    dim3 grid_dim(batch_size);
    dim3 block_dim(THREADS_PER_BLOCK);
    int num_warps = THREADS_PER_BLOCK / WARP_SIZE;
    int shared_mem_size = sizeof(float) * 2 * num_warps; // for smax and ssum
    softmax_kernel<<<grid_dim, block_dim, shared_mem_size>>>(x, y, num_features);

    cudaError_t err = cudaGetLastError();
    if (err != cudaSuccess) {
        printf("Error in softmax_forward_cuda: %s\n", cudaGetErrorString(err));
        return;
    }
}

// PyTorch binding: forward function
torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32.");

    int batch_size = x.size(0);
    int num_features = x.size(1);

    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);
    softmax_forward_cuda(x.data_ptr<float>(), y.data_ptr<float>(), batch_size, num_features);
    return y;
}

// pybind11 module definition
PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Softmax forward (CUDA)");
}
<#sep#>Given \( x, y, z > 0 \), solve the equation \( Sx^2 + 2TTx = 1 \).

<think>
Okay, let's see. I need to solve the equation \( Sx^2 + 2TTx = 1 \) where \( x, y, z > 0 \). Hmm, wait, the variables mentioned are x, y, z, but the equation only has x. Maybe that's a typo? Or maybe S and T are related to y and z somehow? Let me check the problem again. It says "Given \( x, y, z > 0 \), solve the equation \( Sx^2 + 2TTx = 1 \)." So S and TT must be constants that depend on y and z? Or maybe S and T are variables? But the equation is in terms of x. I'm a bit confused. Wait, maybe "TT" is a typo. If it's supposed to be T squared, that would make sense. Like 2T²x. But the original problem says "2TTx". Hmm. Let me think. Maybe in the original problem, S and T are constants, and the equation is quadratic in x. So, assuming S and T are constants (maybe given in terms of y and z, but since the problem just says to solve for x, perhaps S and T are considered constants here).

So the equation is a quadratic equation in x: \( Sx^2 + 2T^2x - 1 = 0 \). Wait, but the original equation is written as \( Sx^2 + 2TTx = 1 \). If TT is T multiplied by T, then it's T squared. So, perhaps the equation is \( Sx^2 + 2T^2x - 1 = 0 \). So quadratic in x.

To solve for x, we can use the quadratic formula. The standard quadratic equation is ax² + bx + c = 0. Comparing, here, a = S, b = 2T², and c = -1.

So the quadratic formula tells us that x = [-b ± sqrt(b² - 4ac)]/(2a).

Plugging in the values, we get:

x = [ -2T² ± sqrt{(2T²)^2 - 4*S*(-1)} ]/(2S)

Let's compute the discriminant first:

Discriminant D = (2T²)^2 - 4*S*(-1) = 4T⁴ + 4S

Then, x = [ -2T² ± sqrt(4T⁴ + 4S) ]/(2S)

We can factor out a 4 in the square root:

sqrt(4(T⁴ + S)) = 2*sqrt(T⁴ + S)

So, substituting back:

x = [ -2T² ± 2*sqrt(T⁴ + S) ]/(2S)

We can factor out the 2 in the numerator:

x = [ 2*(-T² ± sqrt(T⁴ + S)) ]/(2S)

Cancel the 2:

x = [ -T² ± sqrt(T⁴ + S) ]/S

Now, since x > 0, we need to check which of the solutions will be positive.

So we have two possible solutions:

x₁ = [ -T² + sqrt(T⁴ + S) ]/S

x₂ = [ -T² - sqrt(T⁴ + S) ]/S

Looking at x₂, both numerator terms are negative: -T² is negative and sqrt(T⁴ + S) is positive, so adding them with a negative sign gives a negative numerator. Divided by positive S (since x, y, z > 0 and S is likely positive, maybe S is a combination of y and z). So x₂ would be negative, which is invalid because x > 0. Therefore, only x₁ is the valid solution.

Therefore, x = [ -T² + sqrt(T⁴ + S) ]/S

But perhaps we can simplify this expression. Let's see:

Multiply numerator and denominator by [ sqrt(T⁴ + S) + T² ] / [ sqrt(T⁴ + S) + T² ] to rationalize the numerator.

Wait, actually, maybe that's not necessary. Alternatively, let's see:

Let me write the numerator as sqrt(T^4 + S) - T^2. Then, perhaps we can write it as:

sqrt(T^4 + S) - T^2 = [ (sqrt(T^4 + S) - T^2)(sqrt(T^4 + S) + T^2) ] / (sqrt(T^4 + S) + T^2) ) = [ (T^4 + S) - T^4 ] / (sqrt(T^4 + S) + T^2) ) = S / (sqrt(T^4 + S) + T^2)

Therefore, x = [ sqrt(T^4 + S) - T^2 ] / S = [ S / (sqrt(T^4 + S) + T^2) ] / S = 1 / (sqrt(T^4 + S) + T^2 )

Wait, that's a different expression. So x = 1/(sqrt(T^4 + S) + T²). Hmm, interesting. So two different forms:

x = [sqrt(T⁴ + S) - T²]/S or x = 1/[sqrt(T⁴ + S) + T²]

But are these equivalent? Let's check:

Let me take the first expression:

[sqrt(T⁴ + S) - T²]/S

Multiply numerator and denominator by [sqrt(T⁴ + S) + T²]/[sqrt(T⁴ + S) + T²]

Numerator becomes [sqrt(T⁴ + S) - T²][sqrt(T⁴ + S) + T²] = (sqrt(T⁴ + S))² - (T²)^2 = (T⁴ + S) - T⁴ = S

Denominator becomes S * [sqrt(T⁴ + S) + T²]

Therefore, [sqrt(T⁴ + S) - T²]/S = S / [S (sqrt(T⁴ + S) + T²)] = 1/(sqrt(T⁴ + S) + T²)

Therefore, both expressions are equivalent. So depending on which form is preferable. Maybe the second form is better because it avoids subtraction, which can lead to loss of significance if dealing with very close numbers, but in exact terms, both are correct.

Original equation: Sx² + 2T²x = 1

Then rearranged: Sx² + 2T²x - 1 = 0

Using quadratic formula:

x = [-2T² ± sqrt{(2T²)^2 + 4S}]/(2S)

Wait, discriminant is (2T²)^2 - 4*S*(-1) = 4T^4 + 4S = 4(T^4 + S). So sqrt(4(T^4 + S)) = 2*sqrt(T^4 + S). Then:

x = [-2T² ± 2sqrt(T^4 + S)]/(2S) = [-T² ± sqrt(T^4 + S)]/S. So correct. Then since x > 0, we take the positive root:

So the final answer is x<#sep#>human: An infinitely long Line Chillland Collider (LCC) was built in Chillland. There are n pipes with coordinates x_i that are connected to LCC. When the experiment starts at time 0, i-th proton flies from the i-th pipe with speed v_i. It flies to the right with probability p_i and flies to the left with probability (1 - p_i). The duration of the experiment is determined as the time of the first collision of any two protons. In case there is no collision, the duration of the experiment is considered to be zero.

Find the expected value of the duration of the experiment.

<image>Illustration for the first example
Input

The first line of input contains one integer n — the number of pipes (1 ≤ n ≤ 10^5). Each of the following n lines contains three integers x_i, v_i, p_i — the coordinate of the i-th pipe, the speed of the i-th proton and the probability that the i-th proton flies to the right in percentage points (-10^9 ≤ x_i ≤ 10^9, 1 ≤ v ≤ 10^6, 0 ≤ p_i ≤ 100). It is guaranteed that all x_i are distinct and sorted in increasing order.

Output

It's possible to prove that the answer can always be represented as a fraction P/Q, where P is an integer and Q is a natural number not divisible by 998 244 353. In this case, print P ⋅ Q^{-1} modulo 998 244 353.

Examples

Input


2
1 1 100
3 1 0


Output


1


Input


3
7 10 0
9 4 86
14 5 100


Output


0


Input


4
6 4 50
11 25 50
13 16 50
15 8 50


Output


150902884

    71	How to commit
    72	-------------
    73	
    74	In this repository we use the Conventional Commits specification.
    75	The Conventional Commits specification is a lightweight convention on top of commit messages. It provides an easy set of rules for creating an explicit commit history; which makes it easier to write automated tools on top of. This convention dovetails with SemVer, by describing the features, fixes, and breaking changes made in commit messages.
    76	
    77	You can learn more about Conventional Commits following this `link <https://www.conventionalcommits.org/en/v1.0.0/>`_
    78	
    79	What can I push and where
    80	-------------------------
    81	
    82	Our branching system embed some rules to avoid crash of the production environment. If you want to contribute to this framework, here are some basic rules that we try our best to follow :
    83	
    84	-   the modification you offer is solving a critical bug in prod : **PR in hotfix**
    85	-   the modification you propose solve the following issues : test, documentation, typo, quality, refactoring, translation **PR in master**
    86	-   the modification you propose is a new feature : open an issue to discuss with the maintainers and then **PR to develop**
    87	
    88	the maintainers will try their best to use PR for new features, to help the community follow the development, for other modification they will simply push to the appropriate branch
    89	
    90	Create a new release
    91	--------------------
    92	
    93	.. danger:: 
    94	
    95	    for maintainers only 
    96	    
    97	 .. warning::
    98	 
    99	     You need to use the :code:`commitizen` lib to create your release: `<https://commitizen-tools.github.io/commitizen>`_
   100	    
   101	In the files change the version number by runnning commitizen `bump`: 
   102	
   103	.. code-block:: console
   104	
   105	    cz bump
   106	
   107	It should modify for you the version number in :code:`sepal_ui/__init__.py`, :code:`setup.py`, and :code:`.cz.yaml` according to sementic versionning thanks to the conventional commit that we use in the lib. 
   108	
   109	It will also update the :code:`CHANGELOG.md` file with the latest commits, sorted by categories if you run the following code, using the version bumped in the previous commit.
   110	
   111	.. code-block:: console 
   112	
   113	    cz changelog --unreleased-version="v_x.x.x"
   114	
   115	Then push the current :code:`master` branch to the :code:`release` branch. You can now create a new tag with your new version number. use the same convention as the one found in :code:`.cz.yaml`: :code:`v_$minor.$major.$patch$prerelease`.
   116	
   117	.. warning::
   118	
   119	    The target branch of the new release is :code:`release` not :code:`master`. 
   120	    
   121	The CI should take everything in control from here and execute the :code:`Upload Python Package` GitHub Action that is publishing the new version on `PyPi <https://badge.fury.io/py/sepal-ui>`_.
   122	    
   123	Once it's done you need to trigger the rebuild of SEPAL. modify the following `file <https://github.com/openforis/sepal/blob/master/modules/sandbox/docker/script/init_sepal_ui.sh>`_ with the latest version number and the rebuild will start automatically. 
   124

   130

platform linux -- Python 3.9.21, pytest-8.3.4, pluggy-1.5.0 -- /opt/conda/envs/test<#sep#>#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>
#include <vector>

#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 256

// Constant memory for frequently accessed values
__constant__ int d_num_features;
__constant__ float d_epsilon = 1e-6f;

__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y) {
    int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    int tid = threadIdx.x;
    int stride = blockDim.x;
    
    const float* x_row = x + batch_idx * d_num_features;
    float* y_row = y + batch_idx * d_num_features;
    
    extern __shared__ float sdata[];
    
    // Find max using registers first
    float thread_max = -INFINITY;
    #pragma unroll 4
    for (int i = tid; i < d_num_features; i += stride) {
        thread_max = max(thread_max, x_row[i]);
    }
    
    // Reduce max values in shared memory
    sdata[tid] = thread_max;
    __syncthreads();
    
    #pragma unroll
    for (unsigned int s = stride/2; s > 0; s >>= 1) {
        if (tid < s) {
            sdata[tid] = max(sdata[tid], sdata[tid + s]);
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }
    
    float max_val = sdata[0];
    
    // Compute exponentials and partial sums using registers
    float thread_sum = 0.0f;
    float local_vals[8]; // Cache some values in registers
    
    // First pass: compute exponentials and store partial results
    #pragma unroll 4
    for (int i = tid; i < d_num_features; i += stride) {
        float val = __expf(x_row[i] - max_val);
        y_row[i] = val;  // Store intermediate result
        thread_sum += val;
    }
    
    // Reduce sum values in shared memory
    sdata[tid] = thread_sum;
    __syncthreads();
    
    #pragma unroll
    for (unsigned int s = stride/2; s > 0; s >>= 1) {
        if (tid < s) {
            sdata[tid] += sdata[tid + s];
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }
    
    float sum_val = sdata[0];
    float inv_sum = 1.0f / (sum_val + d_epsilon); // Add epsilon for numerical stability
    
    // Normalize with minimal synchronization and coalesced memory access
    #pragma unroll 4
    for (int i = tid; i < d_num_features; i += stride) {
        y_row[i] *= inv_sum;
    }
}

void softmax_forward_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features) {
    // Copy num_features to constant memory
    cudaMemcpyToSymbol(d_num_features, &num_features, sizeof(int));
    
    dim3 block_dim(THREADS_PER_BLOCK);
    dim3 grid_dim(batch_size);
    
    int shared_mem_size = sizeof(float) * THREADS_PER_BLOCK;
    
    softmax_kernel<<<grid_dim, block_dim, shared_mem_size>>>(x, y);
}

torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32.");
    
    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);
    
    softmax_forward_cuda(
        x.data_ptr<float>(),
        y.data_ptr<float>(),
        x.size(0),
        x.size(1)
    );
    
    return y;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Softmax forward (CUDA)");

</think>To solve the equation \( Sx^2 + 2T^2x = 1 \) for \( x > 0 \), we start by rewriting it in standard quadratic form:

\[ Sx^2 + 2T^2x - 1 = 0 \]

This is a quadratic equation of the form \( ax^2 + bx + c = 0 \), where \( a = S \), \( b = 2T^2 \), and \( c = -1 \). The quadratic formula \( x = \frac{-b \pm \sqrt{b^2 - 4ac}}{2a} \) is used to solve for \( x \).

First, we calculate the discriminant:

\[
\text{Discriminant} = b^2 - 4ac = (2T^2)^2 - 4 \cdot S \cdot (-1) = 4T^4 + 4S = 4(T^4 + S)
\]

Next, we take the square root of the discriminant:

\[
\sqrt{4(T^4 + S)} = 2\sqrt{T^4 + S}
\]

Substituting the values into the quadratic formula:

\[
x = \frac{-2T^2 \pm 2\sqrt{T^4 + S}}{2S}
\]

We can simplify this expression by factoring out the 2 in the numerator and canceling it with the 2 in the denominator:

\[
x = \frac{-T^2 \pm \sqrt{T^4 + S}}{S}
\]

Since \( x > 0 \), we discard the negative root and keep the positive root:

\[
x = \frac{-T^2 + \sqrt{T^4 + S}}{S}
\]

Therefore, the solution to the equation is:

\[
\boxed{\frac{\sqrt{T^4 + S} - T^2}{S}}

  /testbed<#sep#>#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>
#include <vector>

#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 256
#define WARP_SIZE 32

__global__ void softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features) {
    int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    int tid = threadIdx.x;
    int warp_id = tid / WARP_SIZE;
    int lane_id = tid % WARP_SIZE;
    
    const float* x_row = x + batch_idx * num_features;
    float* y_row = y + batch_idx * num_features;
    
    // Each thread maintains its local max
    float thread_max = -INFINITY;
    
    // Process elements with minimal divergence
    #pragma unroll 4
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += THREADS_PER_BLOCK) {
        thread_max = max(thread_max, x_row[i]);
    }
    
    // Warp-level reduction using shuffle
    #pragma unroll
    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE/2; offset > 0; offset /= 2) {
        float other_max = __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, thread_max, offset);
        thread_max = max(thread_max, other_max);
    }
    
    // First thread in each warp writes to shared memory
    __shared__ float warp_maxes[THREADS_PER_BLOCK/WARP_SIZE];
    if (lane_id == 0) {
        warp_maxes[warp_id] = thread_max;
    }
    __syncthreads();
    
    // First warp reduces the warp maxes
    if (warp_id == 0 && lane_id < (THREADS_PER_BLOCK/WARP_SIZE)) {
        float warp_max = warp_maxes[lane_id];
        #pragma unroll
        for (int offset = (THREADS_PER_BLOCK/WARP_SIZE)/2; offset > 0; offset /= 2) {
            float other_max = __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, warp_max, offset);
            warp_max = max(warp_max, other_max);
        }
        if (lane_id == 0) {
            warp_maxes[0] = warp_max;
        }
    }
    __syncthreads();
    
    float max_val = warp_maxes[0];
    
    // Compute exponentials and partial sums
    float thread_sum = 0.0f;
    #pragma unroll 4
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += THREADS_PER_BLOCK) {
        float val = __expf(x_row[i] - max_val);
        y_row[i] = val;
        thread_sum += val;
    }
    
    // Warp-level reduction for sum using shuffle
    #pragma unroll
    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE/2; offset > 0; offset /= 2) {
        thread_sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, thread_sum, offset);
    }
    
    // First thread in each warp writes to shared memory
    __shared__ float warp_sums[THREADS_PER_BLOCK/WARP_SIZE];
    if (lane_id == 0) {
        warp_sums[warp_id] = thread_sum;
    }
    __syncthreads();
    
    // First warp reduces the warp sums
    if (warp_id == 0 && lane_id < (THREADS_PER_BLOCK/WARP_SIZE)) {
        float warp_sum = warp_sums[lane_id];
        #pragma unroll
        for (int offset = (THREADS_PER_BLOCK/WARP_SIZE)/2; offset > 0; offset /= 2) {
            warp_sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, warp_sum, offset);
        }
        if (lane_id == 0) {
            warp_sums[0] = warp_sum;
        }
    }
    __syncthreads();
    
    float sum_val = warp_sums[0];
    
    // Normalize with coalesced memory access
    #pragma unroll 4
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += THREADS_PER_BLOCK) {
        y_row[i] = y_row[i] / sum_val;
    }
}

void softmax_forward_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features) {
    dim3 block_dim(THREADS_PER_BLOCK);
    dim3 grid_dim(batch_size);
    
    softmax_kernel<<<grid_dim, block_dim>>>(x, y, num_features);
}

torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32.");
    
    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);
    
    softmax_forward_cuda(
        x.data_ptr<float>(),
        y.data_ptr<float>(),
        x.size(0),
        x.size(1)
    );
    
    return y;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Softmax forward (CUDA)");

However, the original equation would then be f(x²) + f(y²) = [q(x)-q(y)]g(x+y). But since x² and y² are non-negative, f(x²) and f(y²) are zero. Therefore, even if f is non-zero on negatives, in the equation, those values are not used. Therefore, the equation reduces to 0 + 0 = [q(x)-q(y)]g(x+y). So as before, either q is constant or g is zero. So if we allow f to be non-zero on negatives, but zero on non-negatives, then the equation still reduces to the same condition. Therefore, even with f non-zero on negatives, we<#sep#>. Then, the first collision must be the pair with the earliest t_ij among those that actually happened.

So the idea is to consider all pairs in increasing order and compute the probability that their collision occurs and is the first.

Indeed, let me sort all the pairs (i,j) by increasing t_ij. Then, the first pair (in the sorted order) that is active (i.e., their directions are towards each other) will determine the experiment time, and all earlier-pairs in the sorted list that are not active would not interfere, but if they are active, then their collision time is earlier.

Therefore, the pairs should be considered in the order of increasing t_ij. The first active pair in this order will be the collider, and the experiment ends at their t_ij.

The expectation can be calculated by, for each pair in this sorted order, the probability that (a) it is active, (b) and all pairs before it in the sorted list were not active.

Each contribution to E[T] is then the pair's t_ij multiplied by this probability.

This way, the total expectation would be the sum over all pairs, in their sorted order, of t_ij * probability( this pair is the first active one).

Since we can't sort all n choose 2 pairs for n=1e5 (which is way too big), this approach is not feasible unless the pairs can be processed in a way that allows a linear or O(n) approach.

    82	        # set the<#sep#>#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>
#include <vector>

#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 256

__device__ float reduce_max(float val, float* shared_mem, int tid, int stride) {
    shared_mem[tid] = val;
    __syncthreads();
    for (unsigned int s = stride / 2; s > 0; s >>= 1) {
        if (tid < s) {
            shared_mem[tid] = max(shared_mem[tid], shared_mem[tid + s]);
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }
    return shared_mem[0];
}

__device__ float reduce_sum(float val, float* shared_mem, int tid, int stride) {
    shared_mem[tid] = val;
    __syncthreads();
    for (unsigned int s = stride / 2; s > 0; s >>= 1) {
        if (tid < s) {
            shared_mem[tid] += shared_mem[tid + s];
        }
        __syncthreads();
    }
    return shared_mem[0];
}

__global__ void optimized_softmax_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features) {
    int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    int tid = threadIdx.x;
    int stride = blockDim.x;

    const float* x_row = x + batch_idx * num_features;
    float* y_row = y + batch_idx * num_features;

    extern __shared__ float shared_mem[];

    // Compute max value in the row
    float max_val = -INFINITY;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        max_val = max(max_val, x_row[i]);
    }
    max_val = reduce_max(max_val, shared_mem, tid, stride);

    // Compute exponentials and sum
    float sum_val = 0.0f;
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        float exp_val = __expf(x_row[i] - max_val);
        y_row[i] = exp_val;
        sum_val += exp_val;
    }
    sum_val = reduce_sum(sum_val, shared_mem, tid, stride);

    // Normalize the exponentials
    for (int i = tid; i < num_features; i += stride) {
        y_row[i] /= sum_val;
    }
}

void softmax_forward_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features) {
    dim3 block_dim(THREADS_PER_BLOCK);
    dim3 grid_dim(batch_size);
    int shared_mem_size = sizeof(float) * THREADS_PER_BLOCK;

    optimized_softmax_kernel<<<grid_dim, block_dim, shared_mem_size>>>(x, y, num_features);

    cudaError_t err = cudaGetLastError();
    if (err != cudaSuccess) {
        printf("Error in softmax_forward_cuda: %s\n", cudaGetErrorString(err));
        return;
    }
}

torch::Tensor forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32.");

    int batch_size = x.size(0);
    int num_features = x.size(1);

    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);
    softmax_forward_cuda(x.data_ptr<float>(), y.data_ptr<float>(), batch_size, num_features);
    return y;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("forward", &forward, "Softmax forward (CUDA)");

Meanwhile, between B and C:

C is moving left (so direction towards B) and B's direction would need to determine. Suppose B is moving right with p_i=100%. Then, B and C are moving away from each other → no collision between them. 

Between A and B: If B is moving right, then A is behind B moving same direction (so no collision possible with B). So their direction doesn't allow collision. 

Thus, the only collision possibility is A and C, even though they are non-adjacent pairs. But in this case, are they non-adjacent? The pipes are arranged 0,4,5. Example setup:

Consider four pipes:

Pipe 1 at 0, moving right (p1=100%) with speed 100.

Pipe 3 at 3, moving left with (say p3=0% to have direction left), speed 10.

The distance is 3, so their relative speed is 100 + 10 = 110, collision time is 3 / 110 ≈0.027 seconds.

Middle pipes: Pipes 2 is at 1 and 2 at 2. Let them have directions away:

Pipe 2 (position 1) moves right (p=100%, so direction right), pipe 3 (position 3) moving left (so pipe 2 and 3 are moving towards each other, but pipe 2 and 3's collision time would be (3-1)/(v2 + v3). Suppose pipe2's speed is 0.1, and pipe3's is 10 (as given):

then their collision distance is 3-1=2 units, speed 0.1+10 = 10.1 → time 2 / 10.1 ≈0.198 which is higher than 0.027.

Hence, it's possible, so we can't restrict to adjacent pairs. 

So how else could this be approached?

Let me think of each pair's collision time t_ij, and the event that this collision occurs (probability q_{i,j} = p_i * (1-p_j) ). 

So the first collision time will be the minimum of all such t_ij where the collision occurs (i.e., with direction towards each other). 

Note that, the minimum is over variables which are only present (exist) with their certain probabilities. So the expectation of the minimum can be written as Sum_{S} (time of the event giving the minimum in S) * the probability that S is exactly the set of active events, and S's minimum is that time. 

But this is still difficult. 

Alternatively, maybe using the linearity of expectation: consider the expectation can be expressed as the sum over all time t of the probability that the first collision happens at time >t, integrated over t from 0 to infinity.

The mathematics is that E[T] = Integrate_{0}^{∞} P(T > t) dt.

Now, P(T > t) is the probability that for all pairs (i,j), either their collision doesn't occur (i.e., they didn't approach each other), OR they would meet at a time >= t.

Breaking down: for all pairs (i,j), either they don't collide (direction independence: their directions don't allow collision, i.e., proton i moving left or j moving right), or their collision time >=t. 

So the probability P(T > t) can be expressed as the product over all pairs (i,j) of the probability that the collision between them does not happen before time t.

So, perhaps P(T> t) can be expressed as the sum over all possible direction choices of protons (each proton has independent p and 1-p) of the product for each proton's direction. So for each direction configuration, the collision is the minimum of all t_ij where such a collision is possible (i.e., direction towards each other).

Then, T is this minimum for that direction configuration, and the expectation is the average over all possible direction configurations.

    49	        class Test_io(Model<#sep#>#include <torch/extension.h>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>
#include <vector>

#define THREADS_PER_BLOCK 256
#define WARP_SIZE 32

__global__ void softmax_optimized_kernel(const float* __restrict__ x, float* __restrict__ y, int num_features) {
    // Each block handles one row of the input tensor
    int batch_idx = blockIdx.x;
    int tid = threadIdx.x;
    int warp_id = tid / WARP_SIZE;
    int lane = tid % WARP_SIZE;
    int num_warps = blockDim.x / WARP_SIZE;

    // Partition the row among warps
    int elementsPerWarp = (num_features + num_warps - 1) / num_warps;
    int start = warp_id * elementsPerWarp;
    int end = min(start + elementsPerWarp, num_features);

    // Shared memory for reduction, using two sets for storing max and sum values
    extern __shared__ float smem[];
    float* smax = smem;
    float* ssum = smem + num_warps;

    // Compute local maximum and reduction for max in warp using shuffle
    float local_max = -INFINITY;
    for (int i = start + lane; i < end; i += WARP_SIZE) {
        float val = x[batch_idx * num_features + i];
        local_max = fmaxf(local_max, val);
    }
    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE / 2; offset > 0; offset /= 2) {
        local_max = fmaxf(local_max, __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, local_max, offset));
    }
    if (lane == 0) {
        smax[warp_id] = local_max;
    }
    __syncthreads();

    // Max reduction among all warps
    if (tid == 0) {
        float global_max = -INFINITY;
        for (int i = 0; i < num_warps; i++) {
            global_max = fmaxf(global_max, smax[i]);
        }
        smax[0] = global_max;
    }
    __syncthreads();
    float global_max = smax[0];

    // Compute local sum of exponentials
    float local_sum = 0.0f;
    for (int i = start + lane; i < end; i += WARP_SIZE) {
        float val = x[batch_idx * num_features + i];
        float exp_val = __expf(val - global_max);
        y[batch_idx * num_features + i] = exp_val;
        local_sum += exp_val;
    }
    
    // Reduction for sums using shuffle
    for (int offset = WARP_SIZE / 2; offset > 0; offset /= 2) {
        local_sum += __shfl_down_sync(0xffffffff, local_sum, offset);
    }
    if (lane == 0) {
        ssum[warp_id] = local_sum;
    }
    __syncthreads();

    // Sum reduction among warps
    if (tid == 0) {
        float global_sum = 0.0f;
        for (int i = 0; i < num_warps; i++) {
            global_sum += ssum[i];
        }
        ssum[0] = global_sum;
    }
    __syncthreads();
    float global_sum = ssum[0];

    // Normalize using coalesced access within each warp's segment
    for (int i = start + lane; i < end; i += WARP_SIZE) {
        y[batch_idx * num_features + i] = y[batch_idx * num_features + i] / global_sum;
    }
}

// Host function to launch the optimized softmax kernel
void softmax_optimized_cuda(const float* x, float* y, int batch_size, int num_features) {
    dim3 grid_dim(batch_size);
    dim3 block_dim(THREADS_PER_BLOCK);
    int num_warps = THREADS_PER_BLOCK / WARP_SIZE;
    int shared_mem_size = sizeof(float) * 2 * num_warps;
    softmax_optimized_kernel<<<grid_dim, block_dim, shared_mem_size>>>(x, y, num_features);

    cudaError_t err = cudaGetLastError();
    if (err != cudaSuccess) {
        printf("Error in softmax_optimized_cuda: %s\n", cudaGetErrorString(err));
        return;
    }
}

torch::Tensor optimized_forward(torch::Tensor x) {
    TORCH_CHECK(x.is_cuda(), "Input tensor must be a CUDA tensor.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.dim() == 2, "Input tensor must be 2D.");
    TORCH_CHECK(x.scalar_type() == torch::kFloat32, "Input tensor must be float32.");

    int batch_size = x.size(0);
    int num_features = x.size(1);

    auto y = torch::empty_like(x);
    softmax_optimized_cuda(x.data_ptr<float>(), y.data_ptr<float>(), batch_size, num_features);
    return y;
}

PYBIND11_MODULE(TORCH_EXTENSION_NAME, m) {
    m.def("optimized_forward", &optimized_forward, "Softmax optimized forward (CUDA)");

Another approach: Let me note that for any two particles i < j, the collision between them can only be the first one if their collision time is less than any other possible collision between any other pair (k,l) that also occurs (i.e., direction-compatible) before then. 

Therefore, the contribution of a pair (i,j) to E[T] is equal to the probability that:

1. They are moving towards each other (i to the right, j to the left).

2. For every other pair (k,l), if they are moving towards each other (k is right and l is left), then their collision time t_kl > t_ij.

The term contributed by the pair (i,j) is t_ij multiplied by the probability of these two conditions.

Hence, E[T] = sum_{all pairs (i,j)} [ t_ij * Pr( (i,j) is eligible and t_ij is the minimal ) ].

The problem is to compute this sum efficiently.

The question is whether, given all other pairs, how to compute the eligibility condition.

Again, the crux is that n is large, so considering each pair and their relation to all others is challenging.

Wait, perhaps the only pairs that can be the first collision are those for which there is no other pair (k,l) with t_kl < t_ij such that they can potentially choose directions to collide earlier?

assistant: Perfect! This confirms the issues:

1. The DatePicker object doesn't have a `date_picker` attribute
2. The min/max values passed to the constructor aren't applied to the internal date picker
3. The initial v_model value isn't propagated to the internal date picker (it remains None)

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS

### 5.1 Problem Statement
The DatePicker component has three main issues:
1. **Inaccessible internal date picker**: Users cannot directly access the nested `date_picker` component to set properties like min/max values
2. **Non-propagating kwargs**: Date picker specific parameters (min, max, etc.) passed to the DatePicker constructor are not applied to the internal date picker component
3. **Hardcoded initial v_model**: The internal date picker's v_model is hardcoded to None, preventing custom initial values

### 5.2 Problem Location
The issues are in `/testbed/sepal_ui/sepalwidgets/inputs.py` in the `DatePicker` class `__init__` method (lines 51-94).

### 5.3 Test Reproduction
The reproduction script confirms:
- `datepicker.date_picker` attribute doesn't exist
- min/max values passed to constructor aren't applied to internal component
- internal date_picker.v_model remains None even when DatePicker.v_model is set

### 5.4 Best Practices
- Maintain backward compatibility
- Properly delegate properties to nested components
- Allow initialization while supporting dynamic updates
- Follow the existing code patterns in the repository

### 5.5 Fix Strategy
1. **Store internal date_picker**: Make the local `date_picker` variable an instance attribute `self.date_picker`
2. **Process kwargs**: Separate date picker specific kwargs from layout kwargs and apply them to the internal date picker
3. **Handle initial v_model**: Set the internal date picker's v_model based on the DatePicker's initial v_model

## Phase 6. FIX IMPLEMENTATION
